ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e1801	Mr. Federal Minister! My question is: 40/M “Why will the around 2.2 million people who did not have to pay a tax due to their low income be empty in your tax reduction plans, even though this group has been relatively affected by the burdens of recent years?”
ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e1822	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e1837	Mr President, I should like to thank the President-in-Office of Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Mr State Secretary! Mr Hoscher, I should like to say in the first place: We have negotiated a tax reform, and it is therefore essential to note that tax relief can only be effected if tax is actually paid. Nevertheless, I would like to point out that we have made very clear redundancies with the first and second stages of the tax reform, especially in the case of small and medium-sized incomes. You know that we entered into force at the first stage, on 1st. January In 2004, additional 200 000 persons were tax-free and with the second stage we were able to tax additional 150 000 people. This means that of 5.9 million pay and income taxpayers, 2 550 000 people in Austria do not pay wage and income tax. It is a tax reform that relieves taxpayers, on average, €500 net per year, and it is a tax reform that leads to a negative tax increase in some areas, bringing clear reliefs to those 2.2 million people you have addressed. In this context, I may mention vulnerable families, children's-rich families, where we have introduced child supplements to the single-earner and single-parent drop-off rates; for three children, this is a negative tax increase of €525 a year. I would also like to mention the increase in the earnings limit from €4 400 to €6 000 in the single-earner discount amount, and thus we achieved a total increase in the negative tax rate for these 2.2 million people as a target group from €60 million to around €95 million in 2005. Allow me to say that the negative tax on the last tax reform under a Social Democratic Finance Minister has not been increased. I would also like to point out, in an additional sentence, that we have decided, of course, for these 2.2 million people with low incomes, also a whole series of transfer measures, transfer payments, whether this concerns the compensation allowance for married couples, whether it concerns the support fund in the area of care money, whether it concerns the disabled billions, whether it concerns child care allowance or many other things more. The small and medium-sized incomes were the central objective of our commitment to discharge, and to achieve this goal we achieved well with the two stages of 2004 and 2005.

In the final consequence, we have decided that we want to take a particularly large step in corporate taxation, including in the external impact, in order to invest in Austria so that foreign companies come to Austria and thus achieve the greatest possible signal effect or symbolic effect for the economic and labour location. Generics are equally effective drugs, which are cheaper only due to the expiry of patent protection than original preparations. In the field of pediatrics, we have some oversupply, some of which are harsh undersupply. Mr Rasinger rightly repeatedly calls for a children's rehabilitation centre for Austria. This is one of our concerns that we want to ensure during this legislative period.

Dr. Lichtenberger: Mr. colleague, you have not listened!]] This is evidence of a mental attitude that is very clearly documented here. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] But allow me to address the content of this law or reform because it is really necessary and important. Of course, one can easily say that it is acting around a century law. Since we are in the fourth year of a new century, it is easy to say. But if you check it, it really deserves that name. The criminal code is based on the main features of 1873. – I can say straight away: This reform does not concern so few people at all. It's not an orchid theme. I call you only two statistical figures: In 2003, there were 207,000 pending criminal proceedings in District Courts with the Public Prosecutor’s Office, and 106 000 in national courts. That's a remarkable number! This law and this reform therefore affects not only a few Austrians, but a large number of people. In at least 50 percent of the cases, the public prosecutor's office has completed an end. So there were no courts in there. But what has happened so far? Why is this reform necessary? – So far, it has been the case that, while formally the judge was in charge of the preliminary proceedings, he was in charge of the preliminary proceedings. That means he should have led it. In practice, however, it has been crystallized – and there is a real consensus about this – that the investigating judge has been overtaken by police action. The fact is that the executive did what she thought was right and what she wanted, and only went to the judge when she needed something. This situation has been recognized, which is why this reform has become necessary. – That was the one approach to the reform. The second approach to the reform is based on principles of freedom and, in particular, on the protection of victims. In addition – a dispute between the opposition; for us, no issue at all – the accused rights in this context. But let me come back briefly to the history of the origin. – This law, in its fundamentals from 1873, was discussed once in 1998 in the Ministry of Justice with a draft discussion. In 1999 and 2000, there was already a wide-ranging discussion of this draft discussion. In 2001, a ministerial draft was adopted, and in 2002 the new legislative period finally added. Parliamentary deliberations began in May 2003. – If SPÖ Justice Speaker Jarolim here speaks of “Hush-Push”, I have to say, I really don’t understand him. Since May 2003, this law or reform has been thoroughly discussed in the judicial committee and in the relevant subcommittees involving the opposition. Not only did the delegates discuss, but 40 well-known experts were invited. Science, the judges, the prosecutors, the criminal police and the lawyers were invited. I do not understand how – as the opposition does – one can speak of a “huss-puss action”, ladies and gentlemen! [[Abg. Dr. Puswald: Everyone is against it today!]] After this in-depth discussion, the law is finally to be adopted. How has the matter been settled so far, because you criticize it so much? – So far, there has been a three-stage preliminary procedure: a security court preliminary procedure, the pre-examinations of the prosecutor’s office and then the judicial preliminary investigations. This new law now unites this. There will only be a uniform procedure structure. The operational investigation will be transferred to the Criminal Police under the leadership of the Public Prosecutor's Office. The criminal police will be given adequate legal framework for this. Above all, it will receive – and this is also important – modern powers, which is why I would like to mention it at this point too. We were and are currently in some criminal police investigations in the law-free area. The possibility for hidden investigations, for example, is now being standardized. So far, this has only happened in the case of addiction crime. It will now cover all areas of crime. Furthermore, the inclusion of a criminal official into a criminal organisation has so far been held in the right-wing area. This is now being regulated. The conclusion of operations also took place in the right-hand room. We know the discussions: What money do you want to take? How do you write it down? – This is regulated by this. The use of DNA analysis, a very important tool for combating crime, has so far only been regulated by the Security Police Act. It is now included in this criminal process reform. Moreover, observation has not been regulated in StPO until now, except in international contracts. The reform also regulates it in the StPO. Finally, the nature and scope of the identification and physical examination as well as the subject of blood loss are finally regulated. This will no longer take place in the right-hand room. I now come to a very important point, as I have already announced: the rights of victims. Victims of crime are now increasingly protected and supported. This begins with an information participation and application right. Victims will also be informed about the right to information on the procedure and about the file. Above all, there can also be translation assistance – which is important. This is regulated in relation to the victims. Another important area is the following: Victims can participate in party-public evidence and ask questions there. It's very important that the victim has a central role to play. The following is another very important point: Victims of sexual and violent offences are now entitled to psychosocial and legal process support. I can only give an example. An older lady who is robbed is in court against her perpetrator as a witness. She's afraid of it. The perpetrator who robbed her is facing her. Will he get out? Will he be sentenced? – At this point, the care she is entitled to. This is a very important point, ladies and gentlemen. I would also like to mention the request for continuation of the procedure. – I then come to the issue of instructional problems, which is not a problem for us. – The victim is entitled to procedural assistance. This is a very important area. Victims of crime currently receive no free legal assistance in criminal proceedings. That too will change. Finally, I come to speak on claims for damages. If you join as a private participant, there is also the right of proof. In this case too, needy victims should have a right to be accompanied by an attorney. So this refers not only to the accused but also to the victims. This equality of victims is very important to us liberals in this reform. I am now also referring to the position of the accused in the preliminary proceedings, because it is an important point of democracy – not as in the opposition of the most important, but also a very important point. After this reform, the accused will have the right to information on the content of the accusation and the essential rights in the proceedings. At the moment, this is only for him from the point of view of the court's pre-examination – not before. The defendant has the right to have a file, which in principle must already be granted by the criminal police and by the prosecutor. So far, he had only had this in the pre-examination court. Other important points concern the right of proof granted to the accused and the right to translation aid. This was only true in the case of judicial agreements – not yet. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This is a very important matter, namely the enforcement of criminal law and the procedure that belongs to it. This matter is so important because it concerns not only one of the main tasks of government, but also a very sensitive area. Let me now carry this out a little closer. On the one hand, it is about avoiding criminal acts in the interests of all citizens as much as possible, persecuting them effectively and punishing them accordingly, on the other hand, but also about an area – and that is why it is so delicate – in which it is about very massive fundamental rights to intervene in the rights of each individual citizen and of every individual citizen. It is therefore also necessary to take special measures here so that these rights can also be respected and limited only in cases that are really necessary. That is also the intention of this draft. If this draft is compared here today with proposals from experts in the Committee, with basis for discussion – and there are a variety of proposals and wishes, I give you the right – then I must say: I don't think that's right! We should compare this with the current situation in the preliminary proceedings. It is not about the decision-making of the court, but about the investigations on the criminal proceedings, and so far there has been a “grey area” which would be almost too much said – a legal white spot where neither the accused nor his defender had established rights and opportunities. This has often been rightly criticized and has also led to the condemnation of Austria before the European Court of Human Rights. This is now being improved by a quantum leap with this novel and is raised to a level as it is internationally usual and recognized. This was also recognised by many experts in the subcommittee. There are very essential provisions and principles in the criminal procedure on which we are dealing today, and we should take these into account. Firstly, the presumption of innocence must apply; I believe that we all agree on this: It should not be automatically considered guilty of the one who gets into this mill. This is a matter of court proceedings. In the preliminary proceedings, it is a question of setting out investigations that are ultimately intended to provide investigation results, which then form the basis for an indictment, namely that the case is before an independent court, and then it is decided whether a conviction is made or whether the suspicion is not sufficiently hardened. Therefore, the rights granted to the accused are particularly important in the preliminary proceedings. On the one hand, this is the right to be heard, on the other hand, but also the right to contribute to a corresponding expert assistance. I should like to point out that, as a general rule, in more than 90 percent or even 95 percent of cases, it is not a question of lawyers, not of accused persons who are constantly involved in criminal matters, but of those who have no idea of it at all. They should therefore be able to do so. With this draft, they will now receive an expert support from a defender, starting from the first minute. I believe – and thank you for that too – that in the Subcommittee also the representatives of the judiciary stressed that there is nothing against these rights of defence. I should like to point out that this is not about the rights of the defender, even if this is so quoted in the law, but about the rights of the accused to teach people with the appropriate expertise and expertise. This quantum leap in these areas, where we come from a current non-regulation, from the absence of any regulation to a clear legal requirement, concerns the following points: On the one hand, this is the right, as early as possible, to obtain information on the suspicion and also on the essential rights of the accused in the further proceedings. It is, as I believe, an essential point in a rule of law, to be informed of its rights and to be informed accordingly in order to be able to exercise them at all. Only those who are informed can ultimately exercise rights. There is the right to either choose a defender freely or to get a legal aid defender. There is the right – and this too is a complete innovation in relation to the current situation – to take account of files and also to inform ourselves what is at all the basis for the investigations of the Authority, for a persecution by the executive that is taking place here. That wasn't the case until now. The possibility of file inspection has not existed until an act was pending in court. It is precisely in this draft, as we have decided today and discussed it for a very long time, that the right to contact the defender before the first hearing and also to discuss with him, and the right to contribute a defender to the hearing. And there is the right of the accused to apply for evidence. This too is a renewal of the current situation, one, as I believe, in the sense of a balance between the indictment authority on the one hand and the one which is massively affected, very important and very good innovation on the other. A very long and broad discussion was held in the Subcommittee. Of course, there are also case constellations, there are also cases where it is not only a matter of respecting the rights of the accused, but also of being able to effectively identify the relevant investigations. A very important compromise has just been reached. This is what has been so criticised in the approach, also by Mr Stoisits, that, as it were, there should be no possibility at all to restrict the defender to certain information rights and to exclude him from participation. That was just discussion in this subcommittee, which has spent almost a year telling us that there are such cases. This was also explained very clearly by those who are practitioners in this procedure. That is why there is also this possibility of limitation, but – and this is very important – clearly regulated by law, limited in time, and above all there is not a complete withdrawal of defence rights, but in certain cases, if the investigations could be impeded or prevented and if evidence could be restricted or excluded, there is the possibility that the defender may, from the outset, teach the accused about his rights, be involved and attend the hearing, but this first contact is made. monitoring takes place in order to prevent a flow of information beyond other compliments, backmen and so on. I believe that, in the end, a proper solution has been found here between the important interests of the accused, of course, but also the legitimate interests of the state and its citizens, so that crime and, above all, organised crime, which is unfortunately on the rise, can be effectively prevented and properly resolved. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] This compensation, this compromise between the various areas, has been found in the discussions which were quite intense. Each law, one that is discussed and prepared for so long, is ultimately a compromise between different interests. This compensation is fair and will also work, of which I am and of which we are convinced by the government groups. That is why we will be happy to agree to this quantum leap in criminal proceedings today. Thank you.

Some police measures are now being written down by law and thus placed on a legal footing. On the other hand, the relationship between the prosecutor's office and the criminal police is being re-regulated, more precise and modern. Now everyone knows what the other and he himself is responsible for. The new prosecutor is lord of the entire investigation procedure. The new prosecution will also be in the crime scene, and we, i.e. the Kripo, will continue to raise the offences for the prosecutors in the future. Of course, the prosecutor can also determine himself on the spot. In principle, the public prosecutors and Kripo should lead the procedure as far as possible in agreement and produce as possible legal certainty in the investigations. In this case, however, the public prosecutor may in future issue binding orders on Kripo on the investigation steps. There are no longer any personal circumstances, but clear instructions and clear structures. From my professional experience I can say that in practice many ads will remain the same. Most criminal cases, the traditional and small cases will continue to be collected by the Kripo relatively independently and presented to the public prosecutor. Only there are now precise regulations, such as what the Kripo of the Public Prosecutor's Office has to report, in order to guarantee and produce a greater degree of legal certainty and legal competence. It is important in this context to know that, despite the above-mentioned fullness of power, the public prosecutor has to refer the court to the court in the event of fundamental legal intervention. I am convinced that in any case a system has been established that has proved successful in many countries in our community of values and guarantees maximum transparency. The new StPO requires rights and obligations for all. After decades of discussion is finally – I emphasize: finally! – a legislative work, in which the highest degree of legal certainty is safeguarded for the defendants and the affected. It can be built upon the police reform, and in future a modern judicial system will work by a professionally very well-trained and well-organised police apparatus for the safety of our people. ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e9452	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Up to § 17 we are dealing with the procedural principles, but above all with the fundamental rights and human rights of the individual in accordance with § 6 of the European Convention on Human Rights, which are set out here very precisely and also with restrictions which are permitted by all means. I think this is written down in detail, was absolutely overdue. This is an important principle of the rule of law and transparency alone. I'm glad it worked out so well. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] As a result of the command of a fair trial under the European Convention on Human Rights, for example, we have now explicitly enshrined in the law, the right to defence, the right to remain silent, the presumption of innocence, the prohibition of repeated prosecution and many other things that have already existed. They are now also transparently included in the legislative text for the norm subject – a quantum leap in the quality of the legislation. However, it is also particularly important that the division, through which almost as many procedures, as were finally brought to trial in 2002 and 2003, have been completed as a new and important priority: At the centre of the division is finally the reparation towards the victims. I believe that the compensation of victims is of particular importance for the acceptance and sensibleness of criminal proceedings. The first approaches to the disversion procedure we already had could often not be fully enforced. Now, in the context of a judicial comparison, it is possible to also lead execution for the victim to the property of a perpetrator, and it will be possible, far more satisfactory for the population and for the injured than in the past, to carry out this extrajudicial compensation. The payment of a fine may, in addition, also be made conditional on the compensation or payment of a fine to the injured person. The absence of prosecution can also be suspended to a trial period, which will be of great importance for deterrence and prevention. The trial period for the persecution, which we are now considering in the context of the division, will certainly help in many areas of offences to distance itself from a repetition of a deed rather than the previous legal situation. To sum up, I believe that special attention has been paid to the needs of the victims with these new possibilities and that this law rightly allows us to be proud of having a new criminal procedure, which is committed to the rule of law and justice. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e10167	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! We decide today a milestone Mr Wittmann. [[As a result of this, it was not possible to find a solution to this problem. Dolinschek.]] This is a new criminal code, whose now valid precursors essentially date back to 1873! But let me address a group that plays an important role in the criminal process and whose interests are probably the most important: the victims. – The new criminal code clearly extends the rights of victims. It is a pleasure for you, Mrs Stoisits, to recognise this clearly in your minority report. Generally speaking, the role of the parties in the proceedings, i.e. those of the accused and the victims, should be strengthened, so that they are granted additional information, participation and application rights. Nevertheless, one needs to be clarified: The State – and only the State! – has the indictment monopoly. This is one of the most important principles of our legal system and has its good reason because only an objective third party can approach the clarification of matters without reservation. That is why I hold the demand for the possibility of bringing an action for annulment by the private party in the event of an admissibility as too far-reaching, and therefore the claim has not found any precipitation in the reform. It is true, however, that the private party has the right in the future to maintain the charge as a subsidiary prosecutor in the event of the prosecution by the public prosecutor. However, what is provided for in the new StPO is that victims have the right to be informed, to participate in the party's public acceptance of evidence and the main proceedings, regardless of the declaration that they want to participate in the proceedings. They also have the opportunity to question and explain the views of defendants, witnesses and experts, without having to claim damages. Victims will have the right to be accompanied by trial in the future, just as they have so far. The position of the victims as private participants is new, so that they decide to do so. This gives victims a legal status, which has so far been denied to them, in addition to the right of the subsidiary infirmity mentioned above, the possibility of the right of proof – an important progress, especially from a psychological point of view, because the victims no longer feel that it would not have been done all that is possible. Assuming psychological: Emotional victims are also to be entitled to process support, both legally and psychologically, but not according to the principle of foundry, but precisely where it is necessary. For me, the general recognition of the victims of crimes in the trial is decisive, on the one hand by replacing the unhappy concept of the so-called victims with “sacrifice” and on the other hand by § 10 para. 3, which generally emphasizes the obligation that victims should be treated “with respect for their personal dignity and their interest in the preservation” of their intimate atmosphere in law enforcement. However, it is clear that: The suspicion of innocence is the highest legal asset in our country! To demand that no more victims, not even the handbag robbery victim face his perpetrator, went too far. There's a proper process support more effective. However, it is urgently necessary to ensure that the new rights of victims are incorporated into the existing procedure before 2008 in order to enable victims to enjoy the new instruments in this process as quickly as possible. In this sense, our joint resolution is also to be understood in the Committee. This right of procedure is a modern means of finding truth, which on the one hand guarantees the protection of the victims, but also guarantees the rights of the accused and gives the authorities the necessary means to work, with one word: a modern and effective StPO!

[[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We find the corrective in the instance, in the appeal proceedings, because there can be revised mistaken views, but not by the public, not by the media and not by the injured or otherwise by someone else. Ladies and gentlemen! International disasters show that in some cases, even in other countries, no one could be personally held liable. In 1987, for example, there was an accident with a canal journey between England and Denmark. There has also been criminal proceedings for negligent killing, but all employees have been acquitted. Although 193 people have found death, there has been no claim of guilt in the sense of criminal law. The procedure following the terrible train accident in Eschede, in which hundreds of dead were killed, was also discontinued. I would just like to remind you of this because the question arises over and over again, whether the judge may not have spoken a wrong judgment. As there have been discussions and discussions over and over again and the situation that no one has been held for responsibility is unsatisfactory, efforts have been made to create corporate liability, so that legal persons can also be responsible under criminal law. This has already gone a long way within the European Union. There is no such thing in Austria, but there are preparatory work by the Ministry of Justice in this regard. Of course, the question arises what can be achieved by: In those cases where there is no personal debt allocation, where no one is there who can personally be held responsible for an accident, one can draw on the company, which is then also liable for undetectable indebtedness of the employees, e.g. for organisational errors or, if errors occur, the No one can be associated. This also significantly increases the possibility of claims for damages. The Minister of Justice wants to go this way. There are preparatory work on this. So far, however, the economy has been opposed to such corporate liability. However, I believe that we must do everything we can to take account of the international standard as well as the sensitivity of the victims of crime in Austria and to introduce such liability in our country. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! It is not only disasters or major accidents that raise the question of guilty action and the liability of legal persons, but there are also such cases in daily life. For example, events in hospitals or the hospital care scandal in Lainz have also clearly shown that there are cases where there is no individual debt, but there are organizational debts. One comes to the hospital, for example, with a blind arrhythmia, attracts an infection there. No personal debt allocation can be made for this purpose. There is also a Judicatur of the Supreme Court: In such a case, no one pays. The hospital is completely apart from compulsory, although of course the cause of the infection lies in the hospital stay or in the hygiene regulations. I would also like to list some of those examples that have happened in the hospital area, where, despite serious mistakes, no one could be held responsible for criminal law and therefore the claim for compensation has been made infinitely difficult: In January 1997, about a 64-year-old was transferred to a Vienna hospital. Instead of the sick right kidney, the left kidney and spleen were removed. The court proceedings have led to the termination of the procedure against the urologist, but not because the court was sloppy, but because individual debt allocation was not possible. No individual debt allocation could be made. That is, in the normal daily Life, too, has to do with this problem. In Geriatry, we have just recently heard about these terrible conditions in Lainz, where patients are starved, wounded because there was no staff there. It's an organizational debt. It's different if there's a criminal conviction. It is currently true that certain amounts or certain liability standards are set for each damage. For example, if a gas explosion causes material damage outside of a building, the owners of the plant are liable under the Reichshaftsgesetz up to a total height of 750 000 S. Persons injured are entitled to an annual pension of 150 000 S. ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e10791	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! This request has actually made a wound point in the Austrian legal landscape. I am grateful for Mrs Partik-Pablé's support for this, as is common practice among lawyers, with concrete examples, especially in order to illustrate the drama. I will answer this question and assume that there will be some issue in the debate. The fact is that the Ministry of Justice is trying to recognise and respond accordingly to the whole problem. In part, measures have already been taken. Question 1: “How do you assess the current rules on criminal and civil responsibility for hazardous plants and installations?” I can only say this in the sense of the reason for the question: certainly not enough! There is a general liability for hazardous civil service operations, but many detailed questions are open. I call a comparison: A moped, which can only drive 40 km/h, is considered to be a motor vehicle, therefore as a "hazardous operation" – under quotations – and is subject to the Railway and Motor Vehicle Liability Act. However, if a mountain biker with 70 or 80 km/h down the mountain does not apply similar rules for him – neither for snowboarders nor for skiers. This small example in itself shows that the legal regime cannot be reconciled with the social development that has now taken place. We set up a working group in the Ministry of Justice in 2000 under the direction of Professor Dr. Koziol, who has made it a task to revise the entire right of compensation. In criminal law, no liability of legal persons is yet available. We are striving for these; we could be legalized in a few weeks or months and enter the parliamentary process. In total, there is therefore a need to regulate both civil law and criminal law. The law was written at a time when houses were still classified as dangerous establishments and a success liability in the event that a brick fell from a roof. Later on, the Railway and Motor Vehicle Liability Act was introduced. But there are no special rules for much more dangerous establishments, such as hospitals. As far as the operation in hospitals is concerned, the liability is still valid. One will only be able to penetrate as a complainant if one proves to the doctor that there is a lack of information – the unsatisfying answer is the reverse that must be signed – or if, for other reasons, there is a debt of the doctor if, for example, he has not performed an operation of empty artis. – That’s one thing, that’s certainly unsatisfying. For the most part, there is no substitute for intangible damage. Through the EU's pressure, you will now get something that will happen to you on holiday. did not agree and expected, such as a polluted beach, to replace an immaterial damage. However, if, for example, a relative dies in a traffic accident or in another disaster, he is not replaced by any immaterial damage. Pain money can only be paid if you are physically or mentally ill due to the suffering and can also prove this – as was the case with the disaster of Kaprun – by means of a medical certificate. On question 2: “How far have the work of the Working Party on Damage Law been done in the Federal Ministry of Justice?” The general part is currently being drawn up. The next meeting of this working group will be held on 12. ==References== A draft will be available by the end of the year. Judges and lawyers are not yet involved in the discussion. However, it is certain that the risk liability will be extended. Question 3: We've been in the 3. It is probably one of the most fundamental problems. It reads: “Are you going to work to ensure that the specificities of the right of compensation in the event of disasters are taken into account in this working group and that the results so far are reviewed in this light?” We must say that the right of compensation on this point – and this has already been expressed in the explanatory statement of the request – has failed, or is not enough. In the case of Kaprun, the phenomenon is that those companies involved clearly did not understand how to find a human bridge to the survivors of the killed in the context of a corresponding disaster management. It was simply not possible to achieve a human balance. Austria does not get away with international media. If, for example, an aircraft accident occurs in America, where this awareness is already advanced, within hours the representatives of the company are at the place of accident, the survivors personally report the accident to the affected, and subsequently care for it for months, so that it does not result in these uncontrollable feelings that have occurred up to the outbreaks of hatred, as unfortunately was noted in the case of Kaprun. There are also many lawyers involved and responsible because they are called not only to see the legal side but also to include the human side in the representation of their clients. I have therefore agreed with the European Bar Association that we will hold a symposium in September of this year. It will take place in Vienna. I would like to thank the President of the National Council for making the premises available to carry out this symposium. It will be a matter of discussing crisis management and legal advice in the event of disasters in order to improve the behaviour of the companies concerned in similar events. The current situation is characterised by the fact that companies are retreating to the purely legal point of view. In addition, there are statements that really have to be made by the survivors of the killers. Rightly, the victims are claimed to have no pain because of the death struggle only a short time since they were immediately stunned. This, of course, is very difficult for the survivors. Although this is legally understandable, it is not acceptable to people. This symposium is intended to help all of us in legislative innovation to find appropriate regulations that do not make Austria look bad in the world without any reason. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Question 4: "How are you at the recent call for responsibility of legal persons? “ I am, of course, positive about this. In addition to the international implementation requirement, we need to rethink this in the context of the EU. Important as information: As a result, when this scheme is introduced, it may result in both the company and one or more employees being sentenced individually or jointly. Nor does it concern whether one or another can justify the fact that this regulation had already taken effect in the case of Kaprun, for example. It is important that companies in the future know that they can be condemned. For this reason, they will work more carefully in the sense of a general preventive effect of this regulation. In any event, this scheme maintains the principle of indebtedness. The company must make an organisational debt, a supervisory debt, a control debt or similar indebtedness. Without such indebtedness being determined, a company will not be able to be condemned. It's fixed right now. Discussions with the economy have widened. I understand the position of the economy. I understand that it does not want to experience an unsuspected, uncontrollable extension of liability, but we now have to make a regulation in this direction. Question 5: "How do you assess the ratio of judicial criminal law to administrative criminal law in the area of the responsibility of legal persons, particularly in the light of the prohibition of double punishment? “ There will be no double punishment. The responsibility of legal persons will only take place in the area of judicial criminality. It is important to know here that a certain penalty against these undertakings will also be possible. That is already the question 6. On question 6: “What considerations have been given to this in the Federal Ministry of Justice?” As far as sanctions are concerned, there will be fines. The fines against companies will, of course, be higher than the fines against individuals, even because of income conditions. However, there will also be instructions, for example the instructions on compensation for damages – a very sensible consideration and regulation. There will also be the instruction to take technical, organisational or human measures. Question 7: “What is to ensure that the introduction of the criminality of legal persons does not lead to a purely successful liability?” This is ensured by the principle of liability. There will be no deviation from that. Success liability will come in civil law and only there. Question 8: “How do you assess the legal status of patients and caregivers from the point of view of your resort?” As critical as Mrs. Dr. Partik-Pablé and many others, I also judge her. The rights of the same person are too little secured. I thought it was right to set certain standards, which should then be reviewed in court by appropriate processes. Condemnings against a company, a hospital, or a nursing home cause the others to address them. In the field of consumer protection, we have seen that this is very effective. Self-control by the state has not led to success. A follow-up check of the patient's attorneys should also show that the amounts of damages negotiated by the patient are far below what the patients would have received or would have received in the normal court proceedings. This is also a reason to give reason to think about it. On the question 9: “What measures have you already taken in your department to strengthen patient rights or which are in preparation?” I refer to the so far statement, in particular the Home Treaty Act, to the Working Party on compensation for damages and also to the willingness to cooperate with all relevant groups in order to be able to improve very quickly and very effectively in this area. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e11046	Mr. Minister! Mr President. High house! In connection with this tragic event in Kaprun, the process – well-known for the criminal process – there was also an intensive debate on the responsibility of companies. In most cases, the media have not been so clearly distinguished as to whether it is about civil liability, compensation for damages or criminality. I would therefore like to examine these three areas separately. In Austria, we have a very dense civil liability law, a responsibility for companies, and a reform is pending. Thankfully, the Minister has set up a very high-quality working group dealing with gaps or a need for innovation. We also have a very intensive administrative right for companies that provide high penalties in various areas. We are dealing with business activities under administrative law at all. What we don't have for companies is a criminal law in the classical sense. Criminal law for companies does not exist in our system. But it's been in discussion lately. What does that look like? – The debate on sanctions against legal persons is based on various international acts, in particular the Second Protocol to the Convention on the Protection of the Financial Interests of the European Community. It is indisputable that, under these intergovernmental agreements, Austria must also provide for sanctions against legal persons to a certain extent. What are we obliged to do now? – To cover certain crime areas such as fraud, bribery or money laundering. We must penalise legal persons – i.e. companies – if their executives commit offences or if lack of supervision and control of the executives has enabled the criminal action by employees and the act has been committed for the benefit of the legal person. However, sanctions do not necessarily have to be found in criminal law. The EU does not make any regulations here. It is therefore possible to summarize: Sanctions against a legal person are necessary and useful if the offence has been committed for the benefit of the legal person. Austria is free to choose the funds – whether administrative law, civil law or criminal law. The penalties shall be appropriate, effective and dissuasive and shall be required only if there is a full responsibility for doing so. – That means: no penalty without unlawfully guilty act of a manager or employee. How are we now going to implement these European standards? – There have been several scientific studies and also expertise. Professor Lewisch and Parker, for example, have investigated whether there should be a criminal law here, and they reject it Decidedly. The legal question of the introduction of a criminality of the legal person in criminal law is a national issue. There is no obligation under international agreement. As a result, the introduction of a criminality of the legal person is not recommended. It points to constitutional considerations and also points out that there are alternative options in civil law and administrative law that would be more efficient. It then dealt with the issue of the Judge's Week. There, for example, Dr. Marianne Lösnig-Gspandl, on the subject of criminal liability of legal persons, has also said critically that criminal law is problematic for legal persons because the conviction of a company at a penalty is a dogmatic piece of art that testes our criminal law, if not even on the head. Legal persons cannot commit crimes or administrative offences at present. However, there are models of responsibility for legal persons in the wider sense. Such models have then been developed by Dr. Günter Heine, who was published in 2000 in the "Austrian Legal Journal". One of these models of responsibility is linked to crimes committed by the institutions of the legal person. A second model is linked to an organisational debt, to a supervisory responsibility – but again to the institutions. Again, people must have acted guiltyly. The third model provides for an absolute liability of danger, an absolute liability for success. That means, for example: Flood happens, damage to property by flood – criminality. These are things we cannot support. The draft Cedar, which has already been quoted several times – also in the resolution tabled by the SPÖ this morning – and also published in the “Austrian Legal Journal” in 2001, then dealt with how we can get closer to this dogmatic problem. Should we rather regulate this in civil law, in administrative law or in criminal law? – Zeder has opted for criminal law and has also proposed an original responsibility of the legal person. He therefore proposes the model of original responsibility, which resolves to make the criminality of the legal person dependent on a charge against a natural person. The legal person should be responsible for an accusation that he himself has made directly. We have not been able to join this success liability, which is independent from the conduct of the institutions. – We reject that. We say that a liability of the legal person is only imaginable if it is linked to a culpable, illegal and causal act of the institutions or persons or if this debt is in an organisational debt. As always in the field of justice, such major reforms are being prepared scientifically, discussed in the expert group, then sent to the review and finally decided here in the House. [[Abg. Dr. Jarolim: But not as fast as a urgent!]] We certainly do not shoot out of the hip through individual medial reports. I would like to thank the Minister for his careful preparation of this project. We will certainly discuss this again in the House this year.

Now you can discuss what the Minister has also said, whether you should be equal to every case of life, whether there should be standards in this respect, that in any form – whatever happens – man is equally worth it. – I believe that is a well-founded approach that can be taken accordingly. If there is a symposia on this issue, then this is very welcome and will also result in further consequences in your own field, because you can and must learn from certain incidents. The most important point has already been clarified here: We are always talking about the fact that this cannot be a corporate liability according to the motto of absolute liability, but always derived from the principle of indebtedness. And if we assume from the principle of indebtedness that there must be an individual failure, a monitoring or an organisational debt, and that it could not be determined in the specific case of Kaprun, it is clear, of course, that no further consequences can result from liability. We will now see that in the specific case, because here the ECHG – that is, the Railway and Vehicle Liability Act – is important from the liability of the threat. Only when a debt can be determined will it go beyond the certain amounts. The fact that the appropriate choice of words is of course important has already been mentioned several times, and it may not have happened to the extent that it could have been. It is precisely there, however, that we are now on the issue raised here that the economy would object to the indebtedness of doing so. You know very well: Organizations or legal persons always act by people. But if you can't find a person's fault, you can't avoid any undetermined term and say: I couldn't find out that this or that was not done, but there's an organization that didn't do that and did it or wrong, so I generally say: The company should be punished or held liable for this. – This is not in line with our rule of law, especially the principle of indebtedness, and we therefore reject such a procedure! We reject this procedure in particular, because what would be the consequence? – As a result, American conditions would result in the fact that wherever a risk or danger occurs and something actually happens, the liability will first of all be borne, the insurance risk is hardly manageable, and the entrepreneurial performance thus becomes incalculable and no longer manageable. That is why, as an economy, we are actually interested or obliged to act in the same way as is done in the European Union – today, Germany has been addressed several times, where individual indebtedness is the basis for all further consequences and penalties. The second, which is set out in the framework directive or in that Second Protocol at European level, is that the act must be committed for the benefit of the association and that obligations must have been breached accordingly. If you combine all of this with the debt of monitoring, with the debt of organization, then you have a much lower scope for action than it originally looked out. That is why we want to have a decommissioned procedure. It should not be mixed with the other, because otherwise, after each investigation database, one may have the same situation in case of suspicion against the company: that half of the entrepreneurs, the manager, is in court. This would have a very negative effect on the image of the site, and we do not want to. It is also a matter of sanctions. It would be a mistake if the sanctions, the punishments in the criminalistic sense were the decisive thing. The advantage of the company will be decisive if it does not act in accordance with the rules, in contrast to other undertakings. We therefore believe that the sanctions should also go in this direction, namely that: What turns out to be a difference is also to be remunerated as punishment. Next point in this context: It is also necessary to pay attention to the economic viability of the company. If you do not, you will de facto trigger a wave of bankruptcy with such measures if they come too fast and too intensive. And we don't want that either. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: Go, please, don't exaggerate!]] Of course, there should be the following possibility: If companies are to be built up only for the purpose of de facto criminal offences, for example, in the construction sector of various GesmbHs, then in rare cases there should be the possibility that the company will even be dissolved. What I have listed here today is just an exception to our punctuation, which we sent to the Minister of Justice only in February, before the Kaprun judgment. From this you can see that we want to do everything we can to do with this criminal law – let us call it that! – or even with the responsibility of the associations to ensure that a regulation comes out, on the one hand, in line with the interests of citizens, but on the other hand also with the opportunities of businesses in fair competition in Europe.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e13273	Next speaker: Mr Praßl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e13292	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Being a victim of a crime is one of the worst experiences a person can have in his life. This recognition has prevailed in ever larger circles of legal science over the past decades and is now part of the general rule of law. Ladies and gentlemen! In practice, our perpetrator-centered criminal proceedings have led to a forced neglect of the victim over the past decades. The fact that the perpetrators must not be demolished to the mere object of state trade is an ancient rule of law. However, the fact that the victim is more than just a means of proof has not been properly taken into account so far. Specially burdened victims should therefore also receive support, e.g. legal and psychosocial process support. Each victim should have the opportunity to join the procedure as a private participant and in this case has special procedural rights, such as the right of proof. However, it is also new that victims and persons whose criminal interests have been violated by the crime have the right to demand the continuation of proceedings by the prosecutor. If the prosecutor fails to comply with this request, the Oberlandesgericht may refer to the prosecutor the continuation of this procedure. Today's starting point of all considerations is the realization that the victim of a crime in criminal proceedings is to be given a position that acknowledges his own legal personality. These innovations in the criminal code in favour of the victims of crimes correspond to the long-term demand for a continuous sacrifice-friendly exercise of the state criminal claim. Ladies and gentlemen! I very much agree with this reform.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-02-26-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00051_d7e13358	Mr President. Mr. Justice Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! Today is a good day. Why? – For decades it has been tried to change the criminal code starting in 1974, and today a modern criminal process reform is being decided, which has been discussed in six subcommittee rounds involving experts since April 2003. University professors, stand representatives, experienced lawyers, lawyers and experts are the guarantee for this practical reform work. If Mrs. Mag. If, among other things, this reform was called heartless, one would have to face the fact that a quarter of a century would have existed to achieve an improvement for the victims and for the accused. However, this possibility has not been used, although there was a need for action in this area. This government has begun to identify, discuss, teach experts and then implement appropriate improvements. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I would particularly like to address the future role of the Court of Justice in the investigation procedure. The role of the Court of First Instance is in principle limited to the protection of fundamental rights – examples are arrest warrants, telephone surveillance, house searches and complaints. In such decisions, the court may, where necessary, order further investigations by the criminal police. Furthermore, the court may require from prosecutor or criminal police actual information about the case. In addition, the Court of First Instance should also take up evidence if these are not likely to be available in the main proceedings. Apart from the authorisation of force majeure measures, the Court of First Instance shall also act in cases of counter-dictive hearing, and the prosecutor shall apply for judicial investigations or evidence if there is a particular public interest in the prosecution because of the weight of the crime and the person of the suspect. I started with: Today is a good day. Goethe once said: Nothing is harder to endure than a number of good days. – I understand that the opposition This government has provided many good days in this house. Finally, I would like to thank all those who have contributed to the success of this bill, at the head of the Minister of Justice, the chairmen of the committee and all the experts. – Thank you for your attention.
